</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory nipy__nipype__1.0. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
issues with afni.allineate w/ solution (and possible issue with genfile parameter)
###  Summary

Hi all. Just started learning nipype, and it's been pretty great so far. I've only run into issues with the `afni.allineate` interface. I was able to fix it on my own, but I thought I should post an issue here for awareness (plus I'm not sure how to do a pull request...)

There are 2 problems I've encountered. The first is that `afni.allineate` consistently fails the hash check when placed in a workflow and always reruns. I think this is because the `hash_files` parameter needs to be set to `False`.

```
out_file = File(
    desc='output file from 3dAllineate',
    argstr='-prefix %s',
    genfile=True,
    hash_files=False, # <-- when this is added it starts working again
    xor=['allcostx']) 
```
Referencing the docs, I think this is an appropriate change:
> `hash_files`
To be used with inputs that are defining output filenames. When this flag is set to false any Nipype will not try to hash any files described by this input. This is useful to avoid rerunning when the specified output file already exists and has changed.

The second issue is that the prefix (`out_file`) argument does not get generated when used in a workflow. This issue is also referenced here: #2216, but with a temporary workaround.

My understanding is that when `genfile` is set to `True`, the `_gen_filename()` method for the interface gets called when the user does not specify a value. Looking at the code, the `_gen_filename()` does seems to implemented, but not correctly:

```
def _gen_filename(self, name): # this gets called when `out_file` is not defined
    if name == 'out_file':
        return self._list_outputs()[name] # <-- we are returning the value of `out_file`, which is undefined...
    return None
```

So I changed it to this:

```
def _gen_filename(self, name): # this gets called when `out_file` is not defined
    if name == 'out_file':
        return self._gen_fname(self.inputs.in_file,op.dirname(self.inputs.in_file),suffix='_allineate')
    return None
```

Which should define the `out_file` to be in the same location and have the same name as the `in_file`, but with the '_allineate' suffix.

When I run this, the problem seems to be fixed, as the prefix is now defined:

```
[Node] Running "3dallineate_orig" ("nipype.interfaces.afni.preprocess.Allineate"), a CommandLine Interface with command:
3dAllineate -source /home/vana/Projects/p3/tmp/P3/_subject_id_sub-CTS200/3dallineate_orig/orig_out.nii.gz -prefix /home/vana/Projects/p3/tmp/P3/_subject_id_sub-CTS200/3dallineate_orig/orig_out_allineate.nii.gz -1Dmatrix_save FSorig.XFM.FS2MPR.aff12.1D -overwrite -base /home/vana/Projects/p3/dataset/sub-CTS200/anat/sub-CTS200_T1w.nii.gz
```
But downstream nodes don't seem to notice that the `out_file` is actually defined now... (Note: I have  a node whose in_file is connected to the out_file of the allineate node)

```
 [Node] Error on "P3.3drefit1" (/home/vana/Projects/p3/tmp/P3/_subject_id_sub-CTS200/3drefit1)
Traceback (most recent call last):
  File "./preproc.py", line 293, in <module>
    wf.run()
  File "/home/vana/.local/lib/python3.6/site-packages/nipype/pipeline/engine/workflows.py", line 602, in run
    runner.run(execgraph, updatehash=updatehash, config=self.config)
  File "/home/vana/.local/lib/python3.6/site-packages/nipype/pipeline/plugins/linear.py", line 44, in run
    node.run(updatehash=updatehash)
  File "/home/vana/.local/lib/python3.6/site-packages/nipype/pipeline/engine/nodes.py", line 487, in run
    result = self._run_interface(execute=True)
  File "/home/vana/.local/lib/python3.6/site-packages/nipype/pipeline/engine/nodes.py", line 571, in _run_interface
    return self._run_command(execute)
  File "/home/vana/.local/lib/python3.6/site-packages/nipype/pipeline/engine/nodes.py", line 638, in _run_command
    cmd = self._interface.cmdline
  File "/home/vana/.local/lib/python3.6/site-packages/nipype/interfaces/base/core.py", line 935, in cmdline

  File "/home/vana/.local/lib/python3.6/site-packages/nipype/interfaces/base/core.py", line 389, in _check_mandatory_inputs

ValueError: Refit requires a value for input 'in_file'. For a list of required inputs, see Refit.help()
```

After looking at some of the fsl interfaces (and some trial-and-error), I figured out that the downstream nodes seem to be getting the information for their inputs from the `_list_outputs` method of the upstream node, and ignore `_gen_filename`. So when I do this:

```
def _gen_outfilename(self):
    out_file = self.inputs.out_file
    if not isdefined(out_file) and isdefined(self.inputs.in_file) and not isdefined(self.inputs.allcostx):
        out_file = op.abspath(self._gen_fname(self.inputs.in_file,op.dirname(self.inputs.in_file),suffix='_allineate'))
    return out_file

def _list_outputs(self):
    outputs = self.output_spec().get()

    outputs['out_file'] = self._gen_outfilename()

    # other stuff below this ...

def _gen_filename(self, name):
    if name == 'out_file':
        return self._gen_outfilename()
    return None
```

Everything works! But then the question becomes, what's the point of the `genfile` parameter if I have to define a method that checks if the input is defined anyway?

**Problem Summary:**
The `afni.allineate` interface in nipype has two critical issues:

1. **Hash Check Failure**: When placed in a workflow, it consistently fails hash checks and always reruns instead of recognizing when output files already exist.

2. **Filename Generation Failure**: The `out_file` parameter is not automatically generated when not explicitly specified, causing downstream nodes to fail with missing input errors.

**Technical Details:**
- **Method names**: `_gen_filename()`, `_list_outputs()`, `_gen_fname()`
- **Variables**: `out_file`, `in_file`, `allcostx` 
- **Class**: `Allineate` in `nipype.interfaces.afni.preprocess`
- **Error message**: `ValueError: Refit requires a value for input 'in_file'`

**Root Causes:**
1. Missing `hash_files=False` parameter in `out_file` field definition
2. Incorrect implementation of `_gen_filename()` method that returns undefined values
3. `_list_outputs()` method not properly generating output filenames

**Steps to Reproduce:**
1. Create a workflow with `afni.allineate` node without specifying `out_file`
2. Connect downstream node's input to the allineate node's `out_file` output
3. Run the workflow
4. Observe hash check failures and downstream node input errors

**Best Practices for Fix:**
- Set `hash_files=False` for output filename fields
- Implement proper `_gen_filename()` method that generates actual filenames
- Ensure `_list_outputs()` properly returns generated filenames for downstream nodes
- Follow existing patterns from other working interfaces (like FSL interfaces)

    40	Current neuroimaging software offer users an incredible opportunity to
    41	analyze data using a variety of different algorithms. However, this has
    42	resulted in a heterogeneous collection of specialized applications
    43	without transparent interoperability or a uniform operating interface.
    44	
    45	*Nipype*, an open-source, community-developed initiative under the
    46	umbrella of NiPy, is a Python project that provides a uniform interface
    47	to existing neuroimaging software and facilitates interaction between
    48	these packages within a single workflow. Nipype provides an environment
    49	that encourages interactive exploration of algorithms from different
    50	packages (e.g., SPM, FSL, FreeSurfer, AFNI, Slicer, ANTS), eases the
    51	design of workflows within and between packages, and reduces the
    52	learning curve necessary to use different packages. Nipype is creating a
    53	collaborative platform for neuroimaging software development in a
    54	high-level language and addressing limitations of existing pipeline
    55	systems.
    56	
    57	*Nipype* allows you to:
    58	
    59	* easily interact with tools from different software packages
    60	* combine processing steps from different software packages
    61	* develop new workflows faster by reusing common steps from old ones
    62	* process data faster by running it in parallel on many cores/machines
    63	* make your research easily reproducible
    64	* share your processing workflows with the community
    65

    67	-------------

    70	documentation.

   450	class Allineate(AFNICommand):
   451	    """Program to align one dataset (the 'source') to a base dataset
   452	
   453	    For complete details, see the `3dAllineate Documentation.
   454	    <https://afni.nimh.nih.gov/pub/dist/doc/program_help/3dAllineate.html>`_
   455	
   456	    Examples
   457	    ========
   458	
   459	    >>> from nipype.interfaces import afni
   460	    >>> allineate = afni.Allineate()

   463	    >>> allineate.inputs.in_matrix = 'cmatrix.mat'
   464	    >>> allineate.cmdline
   465	    '3dAllineate -source functional.nii -prefix functional_allineate.nii -1Dmatrix_apply cmatrix.mat'
   466	    >>> res = allineate.run()  # doctest: +SKIP
   467	
   468	    >>> from nipype.interfaces import afni
   469	    >>> allineate = afni.Allineate()
   470	    >>> allineate.inputs.in_file = 'functional.nii'
   471	    >>> allineate.inputs.reference = 'structural.nii'
   472	    >>> allineate.inputs.allcostx = 'out.allcostX.txt'
   473	    >>> allineate.cmdline
   474	    '3dAllineate -source functional.nii -base structural.nii -allcostx |& tee out.allcostX.txt'
   475	    >>> res = allineate.run()  # doctest: +SKIP
   476	    """

   555	class AutoTcorrelate(AFNICommand):
   556	    """Computes the correlation coefficient between the time series of each
   557	    pair of voxels in the input dataset, and stores the output into a
   558	    new anatomical bucket dataset [scaled to shorts to save memory space].
   559	
   560	    For complete details, see the `3dAutoTcorrelate Documentation.
   561	    <https://afni.nimh.nih.gov/pub/dist/doc/program_help/3dAutoTcorrelate.html>`_
   562	
   563	    Examples
   564	    ========
   565	
   566	    >>> from nipype.interfaces import afni
   567	    >>> corr = afni.AutoTcorrelate()
   568	    >>> corr.inputs.in_file = 'functional.nii'
   569	    >>> corr.inputs.polort = -1
   570	    >>> corr.inputs.eta2 = True
   571	    >>> corr.inputs.mask = 'mask.nii'
   572	    >>> corr.inputs.mask_only_targets = True
   573	    >>> corr.cmdline  # doctest: +ELLIPSIS
   574	    '3dAutoTcorrelate -eta2 -mask mask.nii -mask_only_targets -prefix functional_similarity_matrix.1D -polort -1 functional.nii'
   575	    >>> res = corr.run()  # doctest: +SKIP
   576	    """

   579	    output_spec = AFNICommandOutputSpec

</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I can clearly see the issues:

1. **Issue 1**: In the `out_file` field definition (lines 218-222), there's no `hash_files=False` parameter. This causes the hash check to fail when placed in a workflow because it tries to hash the output file which may not exist yet.

2. **Issue 2**: The `_gen_filename` method (lines 520-523) is incorrectly implemented. When `out_file` is not defined by the user, it tries to return `self._list_outputs()[name]`, but `_list_outputs()` only populates `outputs['out_file']` when `self.inputs.out_file` is defined (line 491). Since the user didn't define `out_file`, this will cause a KeyError.

3. **Issue 3**: The `_list_outputs` method doesn't generate a filename when `self.inputs.out_file` is not defined, which means downstream nodes won't get the proper output filename.

Looking at the issue description, the user suggests:
1. Add `hash_files=False` to the `out_file` field
2. Fix `_gen_filename` to properly generate a filename when not defined
3. Update `_list_outputs` to also generate the filename when needed

## Phase 4: TEST CREATION - Creating Reproduction Script<tool_call>task_tracker<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>plan</arg_value><arg_key>task_list</arg_key><arg_value>[{"id": "understand_issues", "title": "Understand the specific issues with afni.allineate", "status": "done", "notes": "Identified two main issues: 1) missing hash_files=False causing hash check failures, 2) incorrect _gen_filename implementation causing KeyError"}, {"id": "explore_codebase", "title": "Explore existing patterns in the codebase", "status": "done", "notes": "Found that Qwarp has proper _gen_filename implementation and other interfaces use various patterns"}, {"id": "create_reproduction_script", "title": "Create a script to reproduce the issues", "status": "todo", "notes": "Need to create test that demonstrates both hash check failures and filename generation failures"}, {"id": "analyze_fix_approach", "title": "Analyze the best approach for fixing the issues", "status": "todo", "notes": "Based on user's suggestion and existing patterns"}, {"id": "implement_fixes", "title": "Implement the necessary code changes", "status": "todo", "notes": "Modify preprocess.py to fix both issues"}, {"id": "verify_fixes", "title": "Verify the fixes work correctly", "status": "todo", "notes": "Run tests to ensure issues are resolved"}]</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Task list has been updated with 6 items.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me create a reproduction script to demonstrate the issues:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/nipy__nipype__1.0/test_allineate_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python
"""
Test script to reproduce the afni.allineate issues described in the GitHub issue.

This script demonstrates:
1. Hash check failures when placed in a workflow
2. Filename generation failures when out_file is not specified
"""

import os
import tempfile
from nipype.interfaces import afni
from nipype.pipeline import engine as pe
from nipype.interfaces.base import isdefined

def test_allineate_genfile_issue():
    """Test the _gen_filename issue with afni.allineate"""
    print("=== Testing Allineate _gen_filename Issue ===")
    
    # Create temporary files for testing
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    in_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_input.nii')
    reference_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_reference.nii')
    
    # Create dummy files
    with open(in_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    with open(reference_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    
    try:
        # Test 1: Direct instantiation without out_file should work but currently fails
        print("\n1. Testing direct instantiation without out_file...")
        allineate = afni.Allineate()
        allineate.inputs.in_file = in_file
        allineate.inputs.reference = reference_file
        
        # This should generate a filename but currently fails
        try:
            gen_filename = allineate._gen_filename('out_file')
            print(f"   Generated filename: {gen_filename}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"   ERROR in _gen_filename: {e}")
        
        # Test 2: _list_outputs should generate filename when out_file not defined
        print("\n2. Testing _list_outputs without out_file...")
        try:
            outputs = allineate._list_outputs()
            print(f"   Outputs: {outputs}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"   ERROR in _list_outputs: {e}")
            
        # Test 3: Test hash_files behavior by checking if genfile works properly
        print("\n3. Testing genfile behavior...")
        input_spec = allineate.input_spec()
        out_file_trait = input_spec().traits()['out_file']
        print(f"   genfile attribute: {getattr(out_file_trait, 'genfile', 'Not set')}")
        print(f"   hash_files attribute: {getattr(out_file_trait, 'hash_files', 'Not set')}")
        
    finally:
        # Clean up
        os.unlink(in_file)
        os.unlink(reference_file)
        os.rmdir(temp_dir)

def test_workflow_integration():
    """Test integration in a workflow to demonstrate hash check issues"""
    print("\n\n=== Testing Workflow Integration Issues ===")
    
    # Create temporary files for testing
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    in_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_input.nii')
    reference_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_reference.nii')
    
    # Create dummy files
    with open(in_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    with open(reference_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    
    try:
        # Create a simple workflow
        print("\n1. Creating workflow with Allineate node...")
        workflow = pe.Workflow(name='test_workflow', base_dir=temp_dir)
        
        # Create allineate node without specifying out_file
        allineate_node = pe.Node(afni.Allineate(), name='allineate')
        allineate_node.inputs.in_file = in_file
        allineate_node.inputs.reference = reference_file
        # Don't set out_file - this should trigger auto-generation
        
        workflow.add_nodes([allineate_node])
        
        print("   Workflow created successfully")
        
        # Try to get the node's outputs - this is where downstream nodes would fail
        print("\n2. Testing node output generation...")
        try:
            # This simulates what happens when downstream nodes try to access outputs
            outputs = allineate_node.interface._list_outputs()
            print(f"   Node outputs: {outputs}")
            
            # Check if out_file is properly defined for downstream use
            if 'out_file' in outputs and outputs['out_file']:
                print(f"   SUCCESS: out_file properly generated: {outputs['out_file']}")
            else:
                print("   FAILURE: out_file not generated for downstream use")
                
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"   ERROR accessing node outputs: {e}")
            
    finally:
        # Clean up
        os.unlink(in_file)
        os.unlink(reference_file)
        # Don't remove temp_dir as workflow may have created files there

if __name__ == '__main__':
    test_allineate_genfile_issue()
    test_workflow_integration()
    print("\n=== Test completed ===")</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/nipy__nipype__1.0/test_allineate_issue.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/nipy__nipype__1.0 && python test_allineate_issue.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>=== Testing Allineate _gen_filename Issue ===

**Problem Statement:**
1. **Hash Check Failure**: The `out_file` field in `AllineateInputSpec` is missing `hash_files=False`, causing workflows to always rerun instead of recognizing existing output files.
2. **Filename Generation Failure**: The `_gen_filename` method incorrectly calls `self._list_outputs()[name]` which doesn't generate filenames when `out_file` is undefined, leading to downstream node failures.

**Problem Location:** 
- File: `/workspace/nipy__nipype__1.0/nipype/interfaces/afni/preprocess.py`
- Lines: 218-222 (out_file field definition) and 520-523 (_gen_filename method)

**How Test Reproduces Issue:**
The test shows that `_gen_filename('out_file')` returns `<undefined>` instead of a generated filename, and `_list_outputs()` also fails to generate the filename for downstream use.

**Best Practices for Fix:**
1. Add `hash_files=False` to output filename fields to prevent unnecessary reruns
2. Implement `_gen_filename` to actually generate filenames using `_gen_fname()` method
3. Update `_list_outputs` to generate filenames when inputs are not defined
4. Follow existing patterns from working interfaces like Qwarp

**Fix Approach:**
Based on the user's suggestion and examination of existing code:
1. Add `hash_files=False` to the `out_file` field definition
2. Create a helper method `_gen_outfilename()` that properly generates output filenames  
3. Update `_list_outputs()` to use this helper method
4. Update `_gen_filename()` to use the helper method

    def _gen_outfilename(self):
        out_file = self.inputs.out_file
        if not isdefined(out_file) and isdefined(self.inputs.in_file) and not isdefined(self.inputs.allcostx):
            out_file = op.abspath(self._gen_fname(self.inputs.in_file, op.dirname(self.inputs.in_file), suffix='_allineate'))
        return out_file

    def _list_outputs(self):
        outputs = self.output_spec().get()

        outputs['out_file'] = self._gen_outfilename()

        if self.inputs.out_weight_file:
            outputs['out_weight_file'] = op.abspath(
                self.inputs.out_weight_file)

        if self.inputs.out_matrix:
            path, base, ext = split_filename(self.inputs.out_matrix)
            if ext.lower() not in ['.1d', '.1D']:
                outputs['out_matrix'] = self._gen_fname(
                    self.inputs.out_matrix, suffix='.aff12.1D')
            else:
                outputs['out_matrix'] = op.abspath(self.inputs.out_matrix)

        if self.inputs.out_param_file:
            path, base, ext = split_filename(self.inputs.out_param_file)
            if ext.lower() not in ['.1d', '.1D']:
                outputs['out_param_file'] = self._gen_fname(
                    self.inputs.out_param_file, suffix='.param.1D')
            else:
                outputs['out_param_file'] = op.abspath(
                    self.inputs.out_param_file)

        if isdefined(self.inputs.allcostx):
            outputs['allcostX'] = os.path.abspath(
                os.path.join(os.getcwd(), self.inputs.allcostx))
        return outputs

    def _gen_filename(self, name):
        if name == 'out_file':
            return self._gen_outfilename()
        return None</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>        return outputs

Comprehensive test to verify both fixes for afni.allineate issues:
1. hash_files=False is properly set
2. _gen_filename and _list_outputs work correctly
"""

import os
import tempfile
from nipype.interfaces import afni
from nipype.interfaces.base import isdefined

def test_hash_files_attribute():
    """Test that hash_files=False is properly set on out_file field"""
    print("=== Testing hash_files attribute ===")
    
    input_spec = afni.Allineate.input_spec
    out_file_trait = input_spec().traits()['out_file']
    
    genfile_value = getattr(out_file_trait, 'genfile', None)
    hash_files_value = getattr(out_file_trait, 'hash_files', None)
    
    print(f"genfile attribute: {genfile_value}")
    print(f"hash_files attribute: {hash_files_value}")
    
    # Verify the fix
    assert genfile_value == True, "genfile should be True"
    assert hash_files_value == False, "hash_files should be False"
    print("✓ hash_files attribute test PASSED")

def test_filename_generation():
    """Test that filename generation works correctly"""
    print("\n=== Testing filename generation ===")
    
    # Create temporary files for testing
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    in_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_input.nii')
    reference_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_reference.nii')
    
    # Create dummy files
    with open(in_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    with open(reference_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    
    try:
        # Test direct instantiation without out_file
        allineate = afni.Allineate()
        allineate.inputs.in_file = in_file
        allineate.inputs.reference = reference_file
        
        # Test _gen_filename method
        gen_filename = allineate._gen_filename('out_file')
        print(f"_gen_filename result: {gen_filename}")
        assert gen_filename is not None and gen_filename != '<undefined>', \
            "_gen_filename should return a valid filename"
        assert '_allineate' in gen_filename, \
            "Generated filename should contain '_allineate' suffix"
        print("✓ _gen_filename test PASSED")
        
        # Test _list_outputs method
        outputs = allineate._list_outputs()
        print(f"_list_outputs result: {outputs}")
        
        assert 'out_file' in outputs, "Outputs should contain 'out_file' key"
        out_file_value = outputs['out_file']
        assert out_file_value is not None and out_file_value != '<undefined>', \
            "out_file in outputs should be defined"
        assert '_allineate' in out_file_value, \
            "Output filename should contain '_allineate' suffix"
        print("✓ _list_outputs test PASSED")
        
        # Test _gen_outfilename helper method directly
        gen_outfilename = allineate._gen_outfilename()
        print(f"_gen_outfilename result: {gen_outfilename}")
        assert gen_outfilename is not None and gen_outfilename != '<undefined>', \
            "_gen_outfilename should return a valid filename"
        assert '_allineate' in gen_outfilename, \
            "Generated filename should contain '_allineate' suffix"
        print("✓ _gen_outfilename test PASSED")
        
    finally:
        # Clean up
        os.unlink(in_file)
        os.unlink(reference_file)
        os.rmdir(temp_dir)

def test_user_defined_filename_takes_precedence():
    """Test that user-defined out_file takes precedence over generated filename"""
    print("\n=== Testing user-defined filename precedence ===")
    
    # Create temporary files for testing
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    in_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_input.nii')
    reference_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_reference.nii')
    user_out_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'user_defined_output.nii')
    
    # Create dummy files
    with open(in_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    with open(reference_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    
    try:
        # Test with user-defined out_file
        allineate = afni.Allineate()
        allineate.inputs.in_file = in_file
        allineate.inputs.reference = reference_file
        allineate.inputs.out_file = user_out_file
        
        # Test that user-defined filename is used
        gen_filename = allineate._gen_filename('out_file')
        print(f"User-defined _gen_filename result: {gen_filename}")
        assert gen_filename == user_out_file, \
            "_gen_filename should return user-defined filename"
        print("✓ User-defined filename precedence test PASSED")
        
        # Test _list_outputs with user-defined filename
        outputs = allineate._list_outputs()
        print(f"User-defined _list_outputs result: {outputs}")
        assert outputs['out_file'] == user_out_file, \
            "Outputs should use user-defined filename"
        print("✓ User-defined _list_outputs test PASSED")
        
    finally:
        # Clean up
        os.unlink(in_file)
        os.unlink(reference_file)
        if os.path.exists(user_out_file):
            os.unlink(user_out_file)
        os.rmdir(temp_dir)

def test_allcostx_exclusion():
    """Test that when allcostx is defined, no out_file is generated"""
    print("\n=== Testing allcostx exclusion ===")
    
    # Create temporary files for testing
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    in_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_input.nii')
    reference_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'test_reference.nii')
    allcostx_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'allcostx_output.txt')
    
    # Create dummy files
    with open(in_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    with open(reference_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    
    try:
        # Test with allcostx defined (should exclude out_file generation)
        allineate = afni.Allineate()
        allineate.inputs.in_file = in_file
        allineate.inputs.reference = reference_file
        allineate.inputs.allcostx = allcostx_file
        
        # Test that no out_file is generated when allcostx is defined
        gen_filename = allineate._gen_filename('out_file')
        print(f"allcostx _gen_filename result: {gen_filename}")
        # When allcostx is defined, out_file should not be generated
        assert gen_filename is None or gen_filename == '<undefined>' or gen_filename == '', \
            "out_file should not be generated when allcostx is defined"
        print("✓ allcostx exclusion test PASSED")
        
        # Test _list_outputs with allcostx defined
        outputs = allineate._list_outputs()
        print(f"allcostx _list_outputs result: {outputs}")
        # The behavior might vary, but it should handle the allcostx case properly
        print("✓ allcostx _list_outputs handled")
        
    finally:
        # Clean up
        os.unlink(in_file)
        os.unlink(reference_file)
        os.rmdir(temp_dir)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    test_hash_files_attribute()
    test_filename_generation()
    test_user_defined_filename_takes_precedence()
    test_allcostx_exclusion()
    print("\n🎉 All tests PASSED! import tempfile
import os

# Test basic functionality

"""
Final verification that both issues mentioned in the GitHub issue are resolved:

1. Hash check failures when placed in a workflow (fixed by hash_files=False)
2. Prefix (out_file) argument not generated when used in a workflow (fixed by proper _gen_filename implementation)
"""

import os
import tempfile
from nipype.interfaces import afni
from nipype.pipeline import engine as pe

def test_original_issue_scenario():
    """Test the exact scenario described in the GitHub issue"""
    print("=== Final Verification of GitHub Issue Fixes ===")
    
    # Create temporary files
    temp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp()
    orig_out_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'orig_out.nii.gz')
    reference_file = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'sub-CTS200_T1w.nii.gz')
    
    # Create dummy files
    with open(orig_out_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    with open(reference_file, 'w') as f:
        f.write('dummy')
    
    try:
        # Simulate the exact command from the issue:
        # 3dAllineate -source /home/vana/Projects/p3/tmp/P3/_subject_id_sub-CTS200/3dallineate_orig/orig_out.nii.gz 
        # -prefix /home/vana/Projects/p3/tmp/P3/_subject_id_sub-CTS200/3dallineate_orig/orig_out_allineate.nii.gz 
        # -1Dmatrix_save FSorig.XFM.FS2MPR.aff12.1D -overwrite 
        # -base /home/vana/Projects/p3/dataset/sub-CTS200/anat/sub-CTS200_T1w.nii.gz
        
        print("1. Testing Allineate instantiation without explicit out_file...")
        allineate = afni.Allineate()
        allineate.inputs.in_file = orig_out_file
        allineate.inputs.reference = reference_file
        allineate.inputs.out_matrix = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'FSorig.XFM.FS2MPR.aff12.1D')
        allineate.inputs.overwrite = True
        
        # This should now work without explicit out_file
        cmdline = allineate.cmdline
        print(f"   Generated command line: {cmdline}")
        
        # Verify that out_file was automatically generated with '_allineate' suffix
        assert '_allineate' in cmdline, "Command line should contain auto-generated filename with '_allineate' suffix"
        print("   ✓ Auto-generated filename with '_allineate' suffix")
        
        # Test that hash_files=False is set (this prevents hash check failures)
        input_spec = allineate.input_spec
        out_file_trait = input_spec().traits()['out_file']
        assert getattr(out_file_trait, 'hash_files', None) == False, "hash_files should be False"
        print("   ✓ hash_files=False is properly set")
        
        # Test workflow integration (this was failing before)
        print("\n2. Testing workflow integration...")
        workflow = pe.Workflow(name='test_workflow')
        allineate_node = pe.Node(afni.Allineate(), name='allineate_orig')
        allineate_node.inputs.in_file = orig_out_file
        allineate_node.inputs.reference = reference_file
        allineate_node.inputs.out_matrix = os.path.join(temp_dir, 'FSorig.XFM.FS2MPR.aff12.1D')
        allineate_node.inputs.overwrite = True
        
        # This should not fail anymore
        workflow.add_nodes([allineate_node])
        print("   ✓ Workflow creation successful")
        
        # Test that downstream nodes can access the generated out_file
        outputs = allineate_node.interface._list_outputs()
        assert 'out_file' in outputs and outputs['out_file'], "Downstream nodes should be able to access generated out_file"
        assert '_allineate' in outputs['out_file'], "Generated out_file should contain '_allineate' suffix"
        print(f"   ✓ Downstream access to generated out_file: {outputs['out_file']}")
        
        print("\n🎉 All GitHub issue fixes verified successfully!")
        return True
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"\n❌ Verification failed: {e}")
        return False
        
    finally:
        # Cleanup
        os.unlink(orig_out_file)
        os.unlink(reference_file)
        if os.path.exists(os.path.join(temp_dir, 'FSorig.XFM.FS2MPR.aff12.1D')):
            os.unlink(os.path.join(temp_dir, 'FSorig.XFM.FS2MPR.aff12.1D'))
        os.rmdir(temp_dir)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    success = test_original_issue_scenario()
    exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/nipy__nipype__1.0/final_verification.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/nipy__nipype__1.0 && python final_verification.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>=== Final Verification of GitHub Issue Fixes ===
